                     neural-network training or generic ﬁne-tuning guaran-
                     tees, veriﬁed ﬂoating-point certiﬁcation, uniform re-
                     gret improvement from full curvature, sublinear regret
                     under ﬁxed nonvanishing misspeciﬁcation, two-sided
                     guarantees for arbitrary diagonal, low-rank, stale, or
                     sketched operators, empirical validation of the trans-
                     ported theorem, or end-to-end scalability of the full
                     policy. Ordinary ﬂoating-point residual checks are di-
                     agnostics rather than veriﬁed numerical certiﬁcates.
                     The new experiment is a controlled execution and au-
                     dit, not empirical proof or validation of the theorem.
                     Its endpoint method is a dense diagnostic oracle, and
                     its intentionally naive current-width control is uncer-
                     tiﬁed even where its observed optimism rate is high.
                     Legacy audits are summarized in Appendix N; none
                     instantiates the revised theorem event.

               A.1 A concrete solver certiﬁcate
               Lemma 11 (Inverse-quadratic interval from the original residual). Let C = C⊤  λ I with λ > 0. For an
                                    ˜                                                     ˜         C         C
               approximate solution u to Cu = q, recompute the original residual r = q−Cu. In exact arithmetic,
                                                      ⊤ −1       ⊤˜    ˜⊤      ⊤ −1
                                                     q C q=q u+u r+r C r.                                               (40)
              Consequently,
                                                                                     2
                                                                                  krk
                                                     ⊤˜    ˜⊤       ⊤ −1             2
                                                L=q u+u r≤q C q≤L+ λC =U.                                               (41)
                                             −1     ˜     −1                                        ˜
               Proof. The exact solution is C  q = u+C r. Substitute this expression and q = Cu+r into the quadratic
                                       ⊤ −1                                    2
               form. The remainder is r C    r, which lies between zero and krk /λC.
                                                                               2
               Set sˆ = √U. If L > 0, the observable factor α = pU/L veriﬁes played-action sharpness. If L ≤ 0 < U, the
               interval supplies no ﬁnite positive multiplicative sharpness factor; the solve must continue or an additive-width
               theorem must be used. If U = 0, the exact inverse quadratic form is zero and the solver returns sˆ= 0. For q = 0,
               skip the solve and return zero.
               These certiﬁcates and the approximate-score oracle must use the same realized map sˆ (·). A selected-action
                                                                                                      t
               reﬁnement may keep the original score width as the theorem object, or it must update U (a ) and recheck
                                                                                                           t  t
               the oracle inequality. Fresh randomized reﬁnement or veriﬁcation must be conditioned on the sigma-algebra
               immediately before that draw and assigned failure probability before the draw.
               Corollary 12 (Pointwise CG and PCG solver certiﬁcates). Suppose CG or PCG supplies, in exact arithmetic,
                                                        ˜
               an original-system energy certiﬁcate ku −uk ≤ εkuk with ε < 1. Then
                                                           C       C
                                                               2    ⊤˜            2
                                                       (1 −ε)sC ≤ q u ≤ (1+ε)sC.
              Thus                                         s                 r
                                                               ⊤˜

                                                             1−ε               1−ε
               give a pointwise upper-valid width and sharpness factor for this query. A jointly measurable action-indexed
               family of such solves, together with a simultaneous energy-certiﬁcate event for every scored action, supplies
               the all-domain upper condition in (9); the sharpness conclusion is needed only at the played action. For a
               general standard-Borel domain, measurability of the family and simultaneous functional validity remain additional
               premises. A preconditioner may change the recurrence, but certiﬁcate checks must use the original operator.

               This proves the pointwise two-sided quadratic-form comparison. Dividing its lower side by 1 − ε gives upper
               validity for this query, while combining both sides gives the displayed sharpness factor. Applying the lower
               inequality actionwise on the simultaneous family event gives the all-domain upper condition; only the played query
               uses the upper inequality for sharpness. For PCG the argument is unchanged once the certiﬁcate is expressed in
               the original C-energy norm.
               Neither Lemma 11 nor Corollary 12 turns an ordinary ﬂoating-point residual into a veriﬁed enclosure. Literal
               numerical certiﬁcation requires interval arithmetic, directed rounding with derived norm bounds, or another
               veriﬁed residual enclosure. Float64 point checks remain engineering diagnostics.
               A.2 Measurability, randomized certiﬁcates, and edge cases
               Let J be a ﬁnite or countable certiﬁcate-source index set. For j ∈ J, choose an H−-measurable failure budget
                                                                                                t
               δ   before drawing its random object. Let P   be the pre-reward sigma-algebra immediately before that draw. It
                j,t                                       j,t

===== page 016 =====
                 may equal H− for a round-start draw or include the selected action and earlier randomness for a post-selection
                                t
                 check. It is suﬃcient that
                                                                                  T
                                               Pr(Ec | P ) ≤ δ ,            XXδ ≤δ                  almost surely.
                                                     j,t   j,t     j,t                j,t    cert
                                                                            j∈J t=1
                 The tower property and a union bound give simultaneous validity at least 1 − δ                         without independence.
                                                                                                                   cert
                 Countability makes the joint event measurable, the nonnegative double sum well-deﬁned, and the union bound
                 valid. On an uncountable score domain, the solver upper event must already be simultaneous. A pointwise
                 certiﬁcate cannot be union-bounded after adaptive selection unless it is separately veriﬁed at the selected action
                 with the correct conditioning. Realized terminal spectral quantities may be used for post-hoc analysis, but they
                 cannot be inserted into an earlier action-time radius unless a predictable preﬁx envelope was supplied at that
                 time.
                                                                              ¯
                 The logarithmic theorem remains correct for ﬁnite D ≥ 1, but an exponentially large factor can be vacuous.
                                                                                t
                 Rotating ill-conditioned metrics can have large path length even with ﬁxed eigenvalues; conversely, a closed path
                 can have positive length and equal endpoints. The endpoint Thompson distance ignores the selected path. The
                 Thompson-Finsler length of the selected direct SPD path is factorization-independent, whereas a factor-path
                 upper bound depends on the chosen factorization and interpolation. For data-dependent Fisher weights, an
                 explicit absolutely continuous factor is required: a singular principal square root need not be diﬀerentiable. A
                 uniform positive spectral ﬂoor is essential; positive damping supplies it automatically.
                 B One-sided dynamic fallback: original setup
                 This appendix preserves the earlier Gaussian/squared-loss, ﬁnite-action route. It remains applicable when only
                 one-sided operator transfer is available, but its dynamic realized-width term is not the paper’s headline closed
                 guarantee. The optimizer-centered policy is retained as a secondary specialization.
                 We present the problem setup for the Gaussian/squared-loss instantiation, which is the setting analyzed in
                 Theorem 13. An extension to exponential-family likelihoods is discussed in Appendix L.

              Wewrite θ for the parameter returned by the warm-started optimizer on L . Let D    := {(x ,a ,r ) : s < t}
                        t                                                             t      t−1       s  s  s
              and N := |D    | = t − 1. Equation (43) is a full-history objective. The curvature replay set S ⊆ {1,...,t − 1}
                    t     t−1                                                                         t
              and its size m := |S | aﬀect C , but do not replace D in (43). Thus a generic full-batch evaluation of ∇L
                           t      t        t                     t−1                                                 t
              replays N observations even when m ≪ N . In convex settings (e.g. a frozen backbone with a linear head), under
                       t                        t     t
              exact optimization this coincides with the global minimizer; in nonconvex settings θt may be a local minimizer
              or approximate stationary point. The gap between θt and an idealized global minimizer is absorbed by the
              action-scaled centering certiﬁcate (Assumption 5).

              the damped GGN curvature for the Gaussian/squared-loss model at the current parameter θt. (For the Gaussian
              working model underlying this squared-loss quasi-likelihood, the GGN coincides with the model Fisher information;
              see the remark below.) While C is the natural object for computing predictive variances at θ , it re-evaluates all

                          D One-sided dynamic analysis
                          The motivating scalar-link structure is formalized in Lemma 32, but the proof dependencies run in the opposite
                          direction: we ﬁrst state a master transfer theorem, then its exact-current, spectral-tail, and gap-dependent
                          closures, and ﬁnally the operational and scalar-link specializations. The one-sided fallback theorem analyzes
                          CC-UCB(Algorithm 2, with full enumeration of the K active candidate indices) under the Gaussian/squared-loss
                                                                                                                       t
                          setup and Assumptions 2 to 6. It is not a full exponential-family or generalized-linear-bandit proof; the extension
                          in Appendix L deﬁnes curvature only.
                                                                                                                                                                                     ¯
                          For a general pre-reward SPD sequence C , conﬁdence is proved in the frozen Gram C , whose update and
                                                                                                      t                                                                                 t
                          measurability support self-normalized concentration. A one-sided factor u (a) transfers optimism to C , CG
                                                                                                                                                                t                                                  t
                          contributes α , and nonmonotonicity remains in the realized width complexity. Appendix F.5 gives exact
                                                  t
                          determinant bookkeeping, not a primitive sublinear bound. Later specializations make the nonlinear inputs
                          observable, but their smallness still requires a restricted parameter path.

                Proposition 21 (Tightness of the split spectral-tail expression). Fix integers r,q ≥ 1, masses H,∆ ≥ 0, ambient
                dimension d = r+q, and T ≥ d. Let a PSD d×d matrix have r head eigenvalues equal to H/r, q tail eigenvalues
                equal to ∆/q, and all remaining eigenvalues zero, with the head values ordered no smaller than the tail values.
                Splitting after r makes (73) an equality:
                                                                                 H                ∆
                                                logdet(I +A/λ) = rlog 1+               +qlog 1+           .
                                                         d                        rλ                 qλ
                If ∆/(qλ) ≤ 1, then the tail contribution obeys                
                                                                  qlog 1+ ∆        ≥ ∆.
                                                                             qλ       2λ
                Thus dependence linear in the tail mass cannot in general be removed without additional information about the
                tail rank or spectrum. This is a tightness statement for the information inequality, not a regret lower bound for
                the nonlinear class. The example is algebraic; to embed it in the bounded-feature bandit class one additionally
                                                           2   2
                chooses factors satisfying H + ∆ ≤ TG /σ .
                Proof. The equality follows by substituting the two constant spectral blocks. For x ∈ [0,1], log(1 + x) ≥ x/2;
                apply this with x = ∆/(qλ) and multiply by q.
                A deterministic preﬁx envelope, or one measurable with respect to the pre-randomization history before the
                                           ¯
                round where it is used, ∆      ≥∆ ,mayenter an online conﬁdence schedule through the preﬁx form of (74). A
                                            t,r     t,r
                second valid schedule is (60), which uses the observable full trace for the head and the tail envelope only for the
                tail. It does not subtract an upper tail envelope from the trace. In contrast, a realized terminal ∆T,r is an a
                posteriori path-complexity bound only. Since the inequality is pathwise and simultaneous over the ﬁnite set of
                ranks, the terminal report may minimize over r. Neither the terminal tail nor that post-hoc rank may choose an
                eigenspace, a radius, an action, or any other action-time quantity. If valid predictable envelopes are available for
                several ranks, their pointwise minimum is itself a valid predictable information bound. The existing observable γˆ
                                                                                                                                          t
                schedule remains valid when only realized terminal tails are reported.

                 Remark (Cost of the corrected center). Exact evaluation requires a solve against Ct. A constant number of
                 O(d) CG vectors is insuﬃcient by itself: one additionally needs stored historical frozen gradients, raw replay
                 data with the historical parameter checkpoints needed to reconstruct them, a frozen-feature matrix–vector oracle,
                                                                                                            ¯                         2
                 an explicit low-dimensional representation, or a certiﬁed sketch. Maintaining Ct explicitly costs O(d ) memory.
                 Approximate frozen-feature access introduces an error that must be returned to the centering certiﬁcate unless
                 separately controlled.

                 Remark (Status of Assumption 5). Lemma 25 supplies a valid pre-reward ψ from the optimizer residual,
                                                                                                               t
                 Welford path moments, and observed residual energy. It is predictable when the optimizer-residual bound ζt is

                has action-a mean v⊤tanh(Ux+b)+ca and no W−1/2 forward normalization. With x¯ = (x,1), hidden vector

                 At z = 0 and v        =0its operator norm is kx¯k, independent of hidden width. Thus the executed all-weights

                 parameterization does not imply L ,L = O(W−1/2). Such a conclusion would require a diﬀerent explicitly

                 normalized parameterization and coordinate constraints, which would also change the ridge penalty and GGN
                 geometry. We therefore do not identify W with generic neural-network width or derive optimizer-residual scaling
                 from it.

                 Remark (Linear-subclass lower bound). The exact-rank class contains a standard r-dimensional stochastic linear
                                                                                                                                   √ r
                 bandit. Take a constant context, a known orthonormal basis Q , the ﬁnite action set z(a) ∈ {±1/ r} , linear
                                                                                          U
                 mean µϑ(a) = z(a)⊤ϑ, and Gaussian noise. Choosing the usual hypercube parameter family with coordinates of
                        p                                                           2                                      ¯
                 order    r/T keeps the parameter norm bounded when T & r . Here L = L = E = 0, C = C , and all features
                                                                                               µ      g     T          t     t
                 lie in U. The stochastic linear-bandit minimax expected-regret lower bound is Ω(r√T) in this regime (Dani et al.,
                                               √
                 2008). Hence the leading r T dependence of the exact-rank upper bound is sharp up to logarithms on this linear
                 subclass. This does not prove a lower bound for the nonlinear or spectral-tail classes, nor a computational lower
                 bound.

                     Corollary 33 (Sharp two-sided feature-drift sandwich). If χt < 1, then

                                                                                             t       t                        t      t       t              t     t
                     Proof. Because A⊤At = Id, all d singular values of the (d + nt) × d matrix At equal one. Singular-value
                                               t
                     perturbation and (103) give, for j = 1,...,d,
                                                                             |σ (B )−1| ≤ kB −A k                    =χ.
                                                                                j    t                 t       t op        t
                     For χ < 1, every singular value of B therefore lies in [1 − χ ,1 + χ ]. Squaring the singular values and using
                             t                                             t                                  t         t
                        ⊤          ¯−1/2        ¯−1/2                          2       ¯−1/2        ¯−1/2                    2                                                 ¯1/2
                     B B =C                 CC            yields (1 − χ ) I  C                 CC            (1+χ) I. Congruence by the principal C

                                                                                         2                 2                    2                2
                     proves (116). If 0 ≤ χ ≤ χ¯ < 1, then (1 − χ¯ ) ≤ (1 − χ ) and (1 + χ ) ≤ (1+χ¯ ) , proving the certiﬁcate

===== page 037 =====
               The one-sided fallback theorem uses only the bound (104), which holds for any χt ≥ 0; the smallness condition
               χt < 1 is needed only for the lower half of the two-sided sandwich (116), which in turn is used by the explicit
               near-linear corollary and by any optional analysis that explicitly assumes a two-sided sandwich. An unrescaled
               current-parameter window is handled by Proposition 45 through a one-sided relation and does not require this
               condition. The binding quantity is the whitened ℓ accumulation χ ≤ kD k   of replayed-Jacobian drift, measured
                                                              2               t      t F
                      ¯−1
               in the C   metric—a relative, whitened ℓ bound that can be sharper than an ℓ sum normalized only by λ
                       t                                2                                      1
               when the drift occurs in well-observed directions (it credits directions the design has already explored), though
               it does not dominate uniformly. Together, Lemmas 23 and 31 identify exact whitened diagnostic targets. The
               operational upper bounds in Lemma 25 replace those targets by the predictable path statistics Q and R , the
                                                                                                               t      t
                                                                                     ¯                   2
               certiﬁed optimizer residual ζ , and analytic smoothness constants. Thus ψ and u = (1+χ¯ ) require only O(d)
                                          t                                           t       t        t
               additional state; the exact whitened quantities remain post-hoc diagnostics used to measure the conservatism of
               these certiﬁcates.
                                                   ¯
               Positive deﬁniteness of both C and C follows from λ > 0 and the outer-product form; it is not an independent
                                            t       t
               assumption. All theoretical results hold under these explicit assumptions; we do not claim a regret guarantee for
               the full nonlinear model outside the local-linearization regime.
               F.2   Novelty Relative to Prior Neural Bandits
               NeuralUCB/NeuralTS use historical-time full-gradient Grams in their stated algorithms, while NeuralLinear
               and last-layer Laplace restrict uncertainty to a learned representation or ﬁnal layer (Zhou et al., 2020; Zhang
               et al., 2021; Riquelme et al., 2018; Kristiadi et al., 2020). Matrix-free full-network predictive-variance solves also
               predate this work (Maddox et al., 2021). CC-UCB’s narrower adaptation is to replay every historical Jacobian
               at the current parameter, separate the predictable frozen conﬁdence Gram from that algorithmic operator, and
               expose centering, drift, transfer, and residual-checked CG terms. We claim neither the CG/CVP primitive nor a
               prior assertion of uniform full-metric superiority as new.
               The one-sided whitened result in Lemma 31 is the main technical step: optimism transfer needs no lower spectral
               comparison. The determinant formula is bookkeeping rather than a standalone rate, and the supplied-U closure
               is exactly the projected Gram in (87), not a concrete neural-training theorem. Appendix G compares NTK,
               regression, EKF/LO-FI, structured Laplace, and sketching alternatives.
               F.3   Computable Curvature Operator
               Applying C v = λv+σ−2P          g (x ,a )g (x ,a )⊤v naively requires one curvature–vector product (CVP)
                          t                s<t t   s  s   t  s  s
               per historical sample per CG step—cost O(It) CVPs per round. Each CVP evaluates one Jacobian–vector product
              (forward mode) followed by one vector–Jacobian product (reverse mode) through the network, at roughly the cost
               of one gradient evaluation (one JVP plus one VJP)1; we do not store per-sample gradient features gs, only a
               constant number of O(d) CG vectors for CG, with CVPs computed on the ﬂy. We describe three strategies that
               limit the per-step cost to |S | CVPs:
                                          t
               (i) Subsampled GGN (worst-case spectral baseline). At each round draw a uniform subsample S ⊂
                                                                                                                        t
               {1,...,t−1} of size n (without replacement) and set ws = (t−1)/n to obtain the rescaled operator. The following
               lemma is a worst-case uniform spectral guarantee.

                version follows from the domination argument of Gross and Nesme (2010), building on Hoeﬀding’s reduction
                (Hoeﬀding, 1963): the matrix moment-generating function of a sum sampled without replacement from a ﬁxed ﬁnite
                collection of self-adjoint matrices is dominated by that of the corresponding i.i.d. sum, so every tail bound derived
                from the matrix Laplace-transform method, including the matrix Bernstein inequality of Tropp (2015, Thm. 6.1.1),
                carries over with the same constants. In our setting the {Ys} are ﬁxed PSD matrices (deterministic conditional

                   t           t     t                t                          t      t                  t       t                 t
                              2   2                                                                       2  4   2 2 4          2      2 
                With B = G /σ , the right-hand side of the suﬃcient condition (118) is O L·[m G /(ε λ σ )+mG /(ελσ )] .
                The ﬁrst (variance) term dominates unless ελσ2 & mG2, so this worst-case suﬃcient threshold has a quadratic
                leading dependence on m = t−1 (it is a suﬃcient upper bound on the required sample size, not an exact or
                necessary sample complexity). The condition (118) is conservative and suﬃcient, not necessary, with worst-case
                leading dependence quadratic in m. If its threshold exceeds the population size m = t−1, it provides no nontrivial
                subsampling guarantee for n < m. Taking n = m recovers the full population exactly for the separate reason that
                every summand is included.
                This is a worst-case baseline guarantee, too conservative for practical use at large t. In particular, the bound
                above does not by itself justify computational savings at large t; it should be read as a worst-case spectral
                guarantee rather than as a practical scaling result. Subsampling reduces computation (n ≪ m) only when m is
                moderate or ελ is large relative to mB; for large t the full history may be needed under the uniform Bernstein
                bound. Under additional spectral structure, leverage-score sampling or Frequent Directions sketches may yield
                useful approximations with smaller state. For linear bandits, Kuzborskij et al. (2019) give regret bounds in terms
                of the Frequent Directions spectral-tail error. Translating such structure into a uniform GGN sandwich and a
                concrete buﬀer or sketch size is left open. The Bernstein guarantee is not the mechanism by which we expect
                practical savings at scale. Practical savings require small buﬀers treated heuristically, spectral decay/eﬀective
                dimension, ridge-leverage sampling, Frequent Directions, or empirical veriﬁcation of the spectral sandwich. We
                leave structure-aware sampling and empirical spectral certiﬁcation as future work. To obtain a spectral sandwich
                that holds simultaneously for all t ≤ T, union-bound over t (replacing δ by δ/T). Theorem 46 then exposes the
                one-sided transfer factor and realized width complexity as operator-dependent terms along the realized path; it
                does not identify a standalone causal regret inﬂation.
                (ii) Windowed curvature (unrescaled, current parameter). Maintain a sliding window S ⊆ {1,...,t−1}
                                                                                                                          t
                of the most recent n interactions with unit weights (w =1), re-evaluating the Jacobians at the current parameter θ .

                 F.4     Computational Cost Model
                 Primitive assumptions. CC-UCB requires a single computational primitive: the curvature–vector product
                                                                                                                     ⊤ 
                 (CVP)v7→C v,whichdecomposesintoper-sampleoperationsv 7→ g (x ,a ) g (x ,a ) v . Each such operation
                                 t                                                              t   s  s    t   s  s
                 consists of:
                  1. a Jacobian–vector product (JVP) v 7→ g⊤v (forward-mode AD, cost ≈ 1× forward pass);
                                                                    t
                  2. a vector–Jacobian product (VJP) α 7→ αgt (reverse-mode AD, cost ≈ 1× backward pass).
                 Sharded autodiﬀ can distribute JVP/VJP evaluation, but its communication pattern and sample parallelism are
                 implementation-dependent and are not benchmarked here. For m := |S |, one matrix-free oracle application
                                                                                               t       t
                 consists of m sample-CVPs plus O(d) damping and accumulation work. Thus I                        CGrecurrences for action a
                                t                                                                              t,a
                                                                                                                        ˜
                 use I    m sample-CVPs; an explicit ﬁnal check of the original-system residual g (a)−C u uses another m . The
                       t,a  t                                                                                t        t                    t
                 sequential Krylov state is O(d) (O(Kd) if right-hand sides are batched), excluding model and optimizer state,
                 activations, and raw replay. Caching an explicit m ×d Jacobian matrix instead uses O(m d) additional state
                                                                             t                                              t
                 and is not the on-the-ﬂy JVP/VJP implementation analyzed by this operation count.
                 Krylov methods for Hessian spectral analysis have been run on networks with > 108 parameters under distributed
                 training (Granziol and Juarev, 2026), which shows that related curvature–vector primitives can be implemented
                 at that scale. This does not establish feasibility for the exact per-example GGN JVP/VJP path analyzed here,
                 nor does it measure end-to-end bandit training. Warm-starting CG from the previous round’s solution can reduce
                 the iteration count in practice.

                 Remark (Computability and dimension dependence of βt). Two caveats about βt deserve emphasis. First, γt−1
                                                                           ¯
                 is deﬁned through the frozen-feature Gram matrix C , whose per-sample Jacobians the matrix-free implementation
                                                                            t
                 deliberately does not store; γt−1 is therefore not directly computable in the stated memory model. Second, the
                                                                       2       2 
                 worst-case substitute γ        ≤dlog 1+(t−1)G /(dλσ ) does not exploit low realized information gain and can
                                           t−1

===== page 046 =====
                             be order t when d ≫ t, rendering the resulting bonus and theorem bound vacuous. Corollary 43 addresses both
                             issues with an observable surrogate built from quantities the algorithm already computes; the resulting guarantee is
                             data-dependent, and its tightness still depends on the realized surrogate together with the other theorem factors.

                             This covers an unrescaled current-parameter sliding window, an unrescaled current-parameter subsample, and any
                             current-parameter downweighted subset with weights in [0,1]. It does not cover rescaled subsampling with weights
                             (t−1)/n > 1 (Lemma 34), a stale periodic-refresh operator evaluated at an older parameter, or an arbitrary
                             randomized sketch (e.g. an eigenspace Lanczos surrogate), for which a factor κ+,t must be certiﬁed separately.

                Proposition 47 (Roundwise scalar-width invariance). If two width functions satisfy sˆ (a) = c s (a) for every
                                                                                                              t       t t
                candidate action, where c > 0 is action independent, then replacing a scalar bonus coeﬃcient b by b /c gives
                                           t                                                                           t     t  t
                identical UCB scores and therefore the same selected action under the same tie rule.
                Proof. µb (a) + (b /c )sˆ (a) = µb (a) + b s (a) for every a.
                         t        t  t  t        t       t t
                This is a roundwise statement. If two policies also have identical centers, histories, optimization, and coupled
                exogenous randomness, if ct is predictable, and if proportionality holds at every subsequent common history,
                induction gives identical actions and rewards after the coeﬃcient adjustment. Absent those conditions the
                proposition makes no regret claim. Accordingly, regret diﬀerences can reﬂect anisotropic width distortion or a
                diﬀerence in damping, center, adaptive path, optimization, calibration, or solve accuracy.

                Proposition 48 (Oﬀ-diagonal linear-Gram witness). Let σ = 1, u = (1,1)/ 2, v = (1,−1)/ 2, the action set
                be {u,v}, and θ∗ = ǫu+∆v with ∆ > ǫ > 0. Rewards are noiseless, the ridge is λI, all policies use the same
                full-ridge mean center, and ties select u. If

                (λ,ǫ,∆,b) = (1,0.1,1,1.1) satisﬁes the interval.
                This is a two-dimensional linear-Gram existence witness. For a linear predictor with Gaussian squared loss, this
                Gram is exactly the GGN/Fisher operator, so the construction isolates a GGN-relevant geometric mechanism. It
                is neither a nonlinear-network result nor a uniform dominance result. A dense action-dependent factor recovers
                the full widths, but it uses the reference geometry that the diagonal approximation was meant to avoid.

                What remains open. The path schedules close the computability gap, but their constants can be extremely
                loose. The approximate-rank result replaces exact rank by a realized or predictably bounded spectral tail, but
                does not show that this tail or feature drift remains small during unrestricted training. Useful control of these
                two quantities for a genuinely adapting backbone, tighter smoothness/residual certiﬁcates, and veriﬁed numerical
                enclosures for ﬂoating-point solver and spectral calculations remain open.
                G Related Work
                Positive-cone metric geometry. Thompson (1963) introduced the part metric now commonly called the
                Thompson metric on ordered cones. Nussbaum (1994) developed its Finsler structure and applications. On
                                                                                  −1/2 ˙   −1/2
                the positive-deﬁnite cone, the normalized operator norm kV             VV       k   is the corresponding tangent norm.
                                                                                                 op
                Our contribution is not this geometry or its path-to-endpoint comparison. We instantiate it as an operational
                conﬁdence-transport device linking frozen bandit conﬁdence, current replayed GGN/Fisher curvature, approximate
                operators, and certiﬁed solver widths.
                Neural and linear contextual bandits. LinUCB (Abbasi-Yadkori et al., 2011) and Thompson sampling
                (Thompson, 1933; Agrawal and Goyal, 2013) provide the analytical backbone for contextual bandit exploration.
                Neural extensions—NeuralUCB (Zhou et al., 2020), NeuralTS (Zhang et al., 2021), neural-linear models (Riquelme
                et al., 2018)—use diﬀerent neural feature covariances. NeuralUCB and NeuralTS accumulate full neural-gradient
                Grams in their stated algorithms, while NeuralLinear maintains Bayesian linear uncertainty over learned last-layer
                features. CC-UCB instead re-evaluates every replayed Jacobian at the current parameter and accesses the
                resulting full operator implicitly. Neural-linear designs that train a deep representation but explore only in the
                last layer (Xu et al., 2022a) occupy the opposite design point. Recent alternatives sharpen diﬀerent parts of
                this landscape. Salgia (2023) analyze overparameterized smooth-activation networks through non-asymptotic
                network–NTK approximation and give an eﬃcient sublinear-regret algorithm. Deb et al. (2024) instead reduce
                contextual bandits to online neural regression, using perturbed predictions to obtain their regression guarantees.
                Bui et al. (2025) combine a learned deep representation with a variance-aware linear-UCB exploration layer. These
                are, respectively, an NTK analysis, a regression-oracle route, and a learned last-layer geometry; none analyzes the
                dynamically relinearized full GGN and its CG error. Our low-rank/excitation result is correspondingly narrower
                than generic neural regression, but controls that current-parameter operator.
                Online Bayesian and EKF-style neural bandits. Dur´an-Mart´ın et al. (2022) apply extended Kalman ﬁlter
                (EKF) updates in a learned or random low-dimensional aﬃne parameter subspace. They maintain a dense or
                diagonal covariance in that subspace, inducing a low-rank covariance over parameters spanning all network layers,
                with memory independent of the number of rounds. This diﬀers from applying exact curvature–vector products in
                the full parameter space. More directly, Chang et al. (2023) maintain a diagonal-plus-low-rank approximation to
                the full-parameter posterior precision through recursive EKF and online-SVD updates, and use it for Thompson
                sampling in an MNIST contextual bandit. Their LO-FI method is a one-pass compressed-posterior approach with
                ﬁxed-rank cost linear in parameter count and no replay of past data. CC-UCB instead relinearizes the full replay
                history at the current parameter and accesses the uncompressed GGN through residual-checked CG. Their bandit
                study is empirical; our conditional UCB analysis explicitly accounts for CG truncation, but does not establish a
                practical advantage over LO-FI.
                Last-layer and structured Laplace approximations. Last-layer Laplace ﬁxes the backbone and restricts
                the approximate posterior to the ﬁnal layer (Kristiadi et al., 2020), omitting backbone-weight uncertainty by
                construction. This is only one scalable option: all-layer block-diagonal Kronecker curvature has also been used
                (Ritter et al., 2018), and Daxberger et al. (2021) systematize all-weight, subnetwork, and last-layer variants with
                full, low-rank, KFAC, or diagonal curvature. CC-UCB uses the uncompressed current-parameter GGN as its
                reference operator without materializing the covariance matrix.
                Sampling-based full-parameter exploration. Langevin Monte Carlo Thompson sampling (LMC-TS) (Xu
                et al., 2022b) explores with approximate samples of a loss-deﬁned parameter posterior, obtained by noisy gradient
                steps on the regularized loss; like CC-UCB it never materializes a covariance matrix. Its regret guarantee is for
                linear contextual bandits; its neural-bandit evidence is empirical. The two routes pay for exploration diﬀerently:
                LMC-TS through the mixing of a per-round sampling chain, CC-UCB through CG truncation and spectral

===== page 049 =====
                distortion of an explicit curvature metric, which yields deterministic widths, an optimism-style analysis, and a
                ﬁxed curvature operator reused across separate per-action CG solves.
                Eﬃcient nonlinear UCB. Kassraie and Krause (2022) analyze NTK-UCB and propose NN-UCB and its
                convolutional variant CNN-UCB. NN-UCB combines a trained-network mean with conﬁdence widths from the
                empirical NTK feature map at initialization. They prove sublinear regret for the SupNN-UCB variant under
                NTK-RKHS, suﬃcient-exploration, and network-width assumptions. Their ﬁxed-feature uncertainty diﬀers from
                CC-UCB’s replayed GGN/Fisher curvature at the current parameter θt, accessed matrix-free via CG. CC-UCB
                thus targets the regime where the practitioner has a speciﬁc pretrained model and wants to use its curvature
                as-is, rather than constructing a surrogate NTK-based estimator.
                Spectral sketching for linear bandits.          Kuzborskij et al. (2019) analyze linear bandits where the Gram matrix
                is replaced by a Frequent Directions sketch, deriving regret bounds that depend on the spectral approximation
                quality. Wen et al. (2026) show that ﬁxed-size sketch regret bounds can degenerate to the trivial linear rate under
                heavy spectral tails, and propose an adaptive dyadic block sketch. Our spectral-distortion theorem (Theorem 46)
                instantiates the same conceptual lever in the matrix-free GGN/CVP setting by exposing transfer and realized-
                width terms along the realized trajectory. We do not claim novelty for the sandwich-to-regret reduction itself; the
                contribution is adapting it to the CG/GGN context with explicit CG truncation and feature-drift factors.
                Laplace approximation in deep learning. The Laplace approximation (MacKay, 1992; Ritter et al., 2018)
                provides a Gaussian posterior centered at a MAP estimate with covariance given by the inverse curvature. Scalable
                variants use diagonal, KFAC (Martens and Grosse, 2015), or subnetwork approximations (Daxberger et al., 2021).
                Matrix-free full-Jacobian/Fisher predictive variance using implicit products and CG predates this work (Maddox
                et al., 2021); CC-UCB adapts that computational route to an online, current-relinearized GGN operator and
                supplies bandit-speciﬁc transfer and solver-error accounting.
                GGN–vector products, Hessian spectral analysis, and inﬂuence functions. Pearlmutter’s algorithm
                (Pearlmutter, 1994) computes exact Hessian–vector products; the analogous GGN–vector product has the
                same Jacobian-transpose-times-Jacobian-times-vector structure (Schraudolph, 2002). Recent work on Krylov
                methods for Hessian spectral analysis (Granziol and Juarev, 2026) demonstrates related distributed HVP/Krylov
                                          8
                infrastructure at > 10 -parameter scale, but does not benchmark the exact per-example GGN operator or
                end-to-end CC-UCB latency. Inﬂuence functions (Koh and Liang, 2017) use inverse-curvature–vector products
                to attribute predictions to training points; the CG-based implicit solve we employ is the same computational
                primitive.
                                                                                                                                        ¯
                Optimal experimental design. Information-theoretic exploration objectives—such as maximizing logdet(C )
                                                                                                                                          t
                                               ¯−1
                or minimizing the trace of C        —connect bandit exploration to the classical D-optimal and A-optimal design
                                                 t
                criteria (Valko et al., 2013). CC-UCB’s use of the full GGN/Fisher curvature enables stochastic estimates of
                these quantities via Hutchinson-type trace estimators (Ubaru et al., 2017), without forming the matrix.
                Filtration.     Weseparate pre-reward randomness, revealed before the reward, from post-reward randomness,
                drawn only after rt is observed. Let Ωt collect all pre-reward algorithmic randomness used at round t, revealed
                in stages. Curvature subsampling indices, randomized sketches, and stochastic solver seeds used to construct
                the score are revealed before action selection. The randomized sketch or subsample that deﬁnes the curvature
                operator is then ﬁxed before action selection; it is not re-randomized during any conjugate-gradient (CG) solve
                at round t. (Standard CG requires a ﬁxed symmetric positive deﬁnite matrix–vector oracle across its iterations
                and does not apply to a stochastic oracle that changes between iterations.) Optional selected-action veriﬁcation
                randomness may be revealed after a provisional selection but before the reward, subject to the same-width and
                oracle-recheck requirements in Assumption 1. Let Ut+1 collect the post-reward randomness used to form the next
                parameter estimate θt+1 (minibatch order, dropout, perturbed warm starts). Deﬁne F0 = σ(θ1) and, for t ≥ 1,

                       Remark (Fisher, empirical Fisher, and GGN). Three curvature objects appear in the literature and must be
                       distinguished. The empirical Fisher is the realized score outer product P ∇ logp(r |x ,a ;θ)∇ logp(r |
                                                                                                        s   θ       s   s   s      θ       s
                       xs,as;θ)⊤; it depends on observed rewards and is not the operator used by CC-UCB. The true (model) Fisher
                       is the model expectation of the score outer product, F(θ) = E                  [∇ logp ∇ logp⊤]. The generalized
                                                                                           r∼p(·|x,a;θ) θ         θ
                      Gauss–Newton (GGN) drops the residual-times-Hessian term from the full Hessian, retaining only the outer-
                       product Jacobian part that is guaranteed PSD (Martens, 2020; Schraudolph, 2002). For the ﬁxed-variance
                       Gaussian working model underlying the squared-loss quasi-likelihood used here, the model Fisher and GGN
                       are identical, and both reduce to the outer-product curvature σ−2P gsg⊤ used in (45). More generally, for
                                                                                                  s    s
                       exponential-family negative log-likelihoods parameterized by natural parameters zθ(x,a), the true Fisher equals
                                    ⊤ 2                                        2
                       the GGN: J ∇ A(z)J , where J = ∇ z and ∇ A is the Hessian of the log-partition function (Martens,
                                    θ           θ           θ      θ θ
                       2020; Pascanu and Bengio, 2013). Bernoulli rewards yield scalar weight p(1−p); categorical rewards yield
                       Diag(p)−pp⊤. See Appendix L for the extension.

                 Per-round CG tolerance ε¯. The algorithm uses a known per-round tolerance ε¯ ∈ (0,1) in the UCB bonus
                                                    t                                                              t
                 (line 9), not the true per-action energy-norm errors εt,a (which are unobservable without the exact solves). It
                 suﬃces that max           ε    ≤ε¯ at every round ((50); zero-gradient actions have ε              =0byconvention and are
                                     a∈A t,a        t                                                           t,a
                                          t
                 trivially within tolerance); the bonus is then conservative. Two strategies ensure this:
                  1. Adaptive residual stopping (recommended). In exact arithmetic, for each action monitor the computable
                     Euclidean relative residual kr k/kg (a)k at each CG step (skipping actions with g (a) = 0). Since ε                         ≤
                     √                                  I      t                                                       t                     t,a
                       κ¯  · kr k/kg (a)k (standard CG bound, with κ¯ a certiﬁed upper bound on the condition number of C ),
                         t    I      t                                   √ t                                                                    t
                     stopping that solve when kr k/kg (a)k ≤ ε¯ / κ¯ guarantees ε               ≤ε¯, hence (50). This is conservative by the
                             √                       I      t         t     t               t,a     t
                     factor    κ¯  but requires no access to C−1 and adapts automatically to the per-round condition number. A
                                 t                                t
                     ﬂoating-point implementation needs a veriﬁed residual enclosure to inherit this certiﬁcation literally.
                  2. Fixed budget (requires a uniform condition-number bound). If an a priori bound κ¯ ≤ κ                          holds for all
                                                                                                                        t      max
                     rounds t ≤ T, choose I via Eq. (152) at κ              and a ﬁxed target ε¯ ≡ ε¯. For a nonnegative weighted outer-
                                                           P           max                         t
                     product operator C = λI + σ−2                 w g (x ,a )g (x ,a )⊤ the condition number is bounded in closed
                                            t                 s∈St   s  t   s   s   t  s   s
                     form, κ¯ ≤ 1 +W G2/(λσ2) with W = P                    w ; a ﬁxed buﬀer of size n (W = n, unrescaled) makes this
                              t           t                    t       s∈St   s                                 t
                     independent of T. For the full-history operator (W = t−1), and for rescaled uniform subsampling with
                                                 P                                 t
                     w =(t−1)/n (so W =                  w =t−1), the bound grows with t. For a known ﬁnite horizon, a ﬁxed budget
                       s                     t      s∈St   s                            √
                                                  2     2
                     at κmax = 1+(T −1)G /(λσ ) is valid but scales as O( T log(1/ε¯)); a horizon-independent ﬁxed budget
                     is not uniform, so adaptive stopping or an increasing I is preferable (see Appendix I). For a general SPD
                                                                                      t
                     operator that is not a nonnegative weighted outer product (e.g. a randomized sketch), the closed-form bound
                               2      2

                          Action screening (practical heuristic).                                        When the action set At is large, computing the CG solve for every

                                                                                                                                                                                 t

                Corrected-center remark. Replacing the center µb (a) by the corrected center µb              (a) of (76) sets ψ = 0
                                                                          t                               t                     t
                         ¯
               (so ωt = βt) and removes the centering-stability assumption exactly (Corollary 22), at the cost of frozen-feature
                           ˆlin
                access for θt ; this departs from the O(d) additional curvature-state-memory, no-stored-feature design unless a
                frozen-feature oracle, replay mechanism, or certiﬁed sketch is supplied (see the remark after Corollary 22).
                The remainder of this legacy appendix analyzes the optimizer-centered CC-UCB specialization.
                How to read the legacy one-sided theorem. The theorem is a transfer result for a general pre-reward
                SPD curvature sequence. Lemma 25 and Corollary 17 instantiate its nonlinear conﬁdence, centering, and feature-
                transfer inputs from O(d) path state, observed collection residuals, analytic smoothness constants, and a certiﬁed
                optimizer residual. These schedules are executable but can be very loose. A sublinear guarantee from this bound
                still requires control of the realized dynamic width complexity and a restricted parameter path; unrestricted
                end-to-end ﬁne-tuning is not covered. No horizon-uniform lower transfer is required by Theorem 13; the lower
                sandwich appears only in the explicit near-linear corollary.
                Discussion of terms.
                                                       P
                 • Exploration term. Write S       :=     T  α2u (a )ω2 for the sum of squares of the per-round factors. The
                                         q       T        t=1 t t t     t
                                              2     2      C
                   exploration term is 2    (σ +G /λ)Λ ST. A suﬃcient condition for sublinear regret is
                                                           T
                                                             C           2
                                                            Λ ST =o(T ),         ET =o(T).
                                                             T
                   Concretely this holds whenever the per-round factors are polylogarithmically bounded (sup α2u (a )ω2 =
                                                                                                                      t  t t  t  t
                                                                                                                  C
                   O(polylogT), so ST = O(T polylogT)) and the realized dynamic complexity is small (Λ               =O(polylogT),
                                                                                                                  T
                   e.g. via ΛC ≤ Γdyn with both the variation charge V C and the endpoint logdet(C          /λI) polylogarithmically
                             T      T                                     T                             T+1
                                      2                                                                 ¯
                   bounded). Note ω contains the cumulative squared linearization error through F (via β ) and the centering
                                      t                                                                  t      t
                               ¯               C
                   certiﬁcate ψ , so a small Λ   alone is insuﬃcient. α is bounded whenever the CG tolerance ε¯ is bounded away
                                t              T                        t                                          t
                   from 1; in the exact-curvature case u = (1+χ¯ )2 is bounded whenever the whitened-drift certiﬁcate χ¯ is
                                                           t          t                                                           t
                   bounded (Lemma 31); the whitened ℓ accumulation χ ≤ kD k             makes this a near-stationarity condition on
                                                           2                 t      t F
                                                                                           C             C
                   replayed-Jacobian drift. The unstable-curvature cost is isolated in Λ     (through V ), not hidden in a spectral
                                          p                                                T            T
                                             2     2
                   constant. The factor     σ +G /λ arises from the width-sum bound of the dynamic width-complexity lemma
                                                     2      2                √
                   (Lemma 35, Eq. (123)); when G ≤ λσ it is at most σ 2.
                 • Linearization remainder. The bound involves E = P ε (t), not Tε . Near-linear regimes also require
                                                   P                   T       t lin           lin
                                                             2            ¯                                   ∗
                   the squared-error sum F      =      ε  (t)  (through F , hence ω ) to be controlled. kθ −θ k = O(1/t) gives
                                              T      t  lin             √ t           t                           t
                   E =O(1); kθ∗−θ k = O(t−1/4) gives E = O( T). The additive role of E mirrors misspeciﬁed linear
                     T                  t                       T                                     T
                   bandits, where a uniformly bounded per-round model error commonly contributes an additive regret term
                   that scales linearly in the cumulative approximation error (Ghosh et al., 2017; Lattimore et al., 2020). The
                   displayed bound requires E /T → 0; a nonvanishing uniform per-round error bound alone yields an O(T)
                                                 T
                   additive term.
                                                                                                  ¯     ¯
                 • Centering. The centering certiﬁcate enters multiplicatively, through ω = β +ψ inside the sum of squares
                                                                                              t    t     t
                                                                                  ¯                   ˆlin
                   ST, rather than as a separate additive term. It vanishes (ψt = 0) when θt = θt         (convex last-layer models
                   with exact optimization), and the corrected-center variant (Corollary 22) removes it in general at the cost of a
                   frozen-feature solve.
                 • Regret improvement over diagonal/last-layer surrogates. The bound itself does not prove that full curvature
                   yields strictly lower regret than diagonal or last-layer methods in any speciﬁc regime; it provides an upper
                   bound with explicit per-round factors. Demonstrating regret improvement requires a separate empirical study.
                   No such conﬁrmatory study is reported here. The theorem exposes the transfer factor u and realized width
                                                                                                                t
                                 C
                   complexity Λ    as operator-dependent terms along the realized trajectory; it does not identify a causal regret
                                 T
                   cost because the adaptive path and other factors may also change.

===== page 054 =====
                   • Scope. The bound is proved for Gaussian/squared-loss rewards. Extending it to exponential-family likelihoods
                     (Bernoulli, categorical) would require a weighted frozen-feature analysis accounting for the data-dependent
                                         2
                     Fisher weights ∇ A(z) and is left as future work (Appendix L).

               N Legacy empirical evidence and scope
               Earlier repository revisions contained several studies designed for the optimizer-centered, one-sided dynamic
               theorem. They are summarized here as a scientiﬁc record and are separate from the locked controlled execution in
               Section 6. They are not empirical validation of Theorem 1. None of these historical studies executes the headline
                                                                                          ¯
               exponential transported score while recording a supplied pre-reward Dt, a certiﬁed two-sided operator pair
               κ    , κ  , all-domain solver upper validity, played-action sharpness, and the resulting new regret bound. Most
                 −,t  +,t
               detailed ﬁgures, comparison tables, and protocol chronologies have therefore been removed from the manuscript.
               The two compact tables below retain provenance for their source aggregates.
               Corrected-center and local-transport traces.           The scaled-tanh and bounded-tanh studies record corrected
               centers, frozen information gain, path statistic Q , exact-current widths, and dense or CG solver diagnostics. They
                                                                t
               used scalar-link or additive local scores rather than the exponential score in (10). Their reported PASS/FAIL
               ﬁelds refer only to the legacy theorem event. In particular, optimizer and small-drift ﬁelds removed from the
               new headline theorem cannot be used to declare the revised event failed. The historical scaled-tanh aggregate
               was insuﬃcient for a revised corrected-center analysis because the required per-round payload was stored in an
               external raw bundle. Restoring that bundle would permit a post-hoc audit of the local-small-drift premises and
               of whether the executed score matches the revised local score. It would not establish that compatibility by itself.
               The new policy execution is reported in Section 6; its event and results are not retroactively imputed to these
               historical runs.
               Exact-linear and solver sanity checks. In the ﬁxed-feature linear studies, the dense exact-current and
                                                          ¯
               full-CG rows exercise the specialization Dt = 0. The dense row checks the LinUCB reduction; the full-CG row
               checks original-operator residual logic and ﬂoat64 all-action width agreement against dense solves. These are
               algebra and implementation diagnostics, not veriﬁed numerical enclosures. They do not test nontrivial metric
               transport. Approximate-operator rows record upper comparisons or point generalized eigenvalues, not a reportable
               positive lower certiﬁcate κ   .
                                           −,t
               Nonlinear path and operator mechanisms. The nonlinear drift audit directly checks the corrected-center
               identity and records replay-path statistics, but its schedules fail and its scores predate the revised theorem.
               Oﬀ-diagonal and operator-ablation studies provide diagnostic examples in which operator approximation error,
               width distortion, action disagreement, and regret do not share a total ordering. They motivate separating the
               three metrics in Section 2; they are not theorem instantiations. The spectral-tail study’s rank truncation control
               explicitly sets discarded-coordinate widths to zero. That control cannot be represented by a ﬁnite SPD operator
               satisfying (8).
               Autodiﬀ feasibility.      The real-torch.func study demonstrates JVP/VJP application of a GGN primitive
               and records residual, operator-call, time, and memory diagnostics. It measures isolated linear-algebra primitives
               rather than a transported-UCB policy. The larger model has no dense reference oracle. The study therefore
               supports implementation feasibility only.
               Negative and incomplete benchmarks. The balanced synthetic comparison does not establish a full-
               curvature advantage. The MNIST study remains near its context-free baseline, and Covertype fails its contextual

                   certiﬁcates. The Wheel study terminated during tuning and had no reportable evaluation. The detailed legacy
                   outputs are not part of the compact checkout; Git history preserves them. Their plots and tables do not contribute
                   to the present theory-ﬁrst paper.
                   O Broader Impact
                   Exploration can expose users to under-vetted actions and amplify feedback loops. Deployment requires safety
                   constraints, action ﬁlters, oﬄine evaluation, and online monitoring; the regret certiﬁcates here do not establish
                   safety.
                   P Data and Code Availability
                   The repository contains the manuscript, theory derivations, implementation, tests, frozen transport-instantiation
                   protocol and conﬁguration, optimizer selection record, strict full aggregate, provenance records, and generated
                   tables and ﬁgures used in Section 6. The aggregate is bound to the complete raw-input inventory by SHA-256
                   provenance. Raw run ﬁles remain outside the committed source tree; no public raw-bundle URL is claimed.
                   The manuscript does not use the legacy experiments as evidence for Theorem 1; their scope is summarized in
                   Appendix N. Detailed legacy ﬁgures, raw-bundle inventories, and derived benchmark outputs are not part of the
                   compact checkout; Git history preserves them.
